The second joke of the 88th article of our Constitution states that the draft and proposals of the law are organised in accordance with the rules and principles of meeting in the Turkish Grand National Assembly. The 52nd article of our Civil Code states that a commission report or any text referred to the General Assembly cannot be discussed until 48 hours after the date of distribution, unless the decision is made. For a job to be taken to the top of the agenda without meeting the waiting period, it can only be discussed on the basis and condition of the government or commission. The Constitutional Court will have two conditions for a job to be discussed first and immediately. If we remember how these alliances were made and what the outcome was, the importance and meaning of the arrangement brought with this bill would be better understood. In the 1991 elections, he made an alliance under the roof of the Welfare Party, the National Labour Party and the Reformed Democracy Party's Welfare Party, and overcoming the dam with a 16.88 per cent vote, raising 62 MPs. 19 members of the National Labour Party and 3 members of the Reformed Democracy Party returned to their own party after the election. In the 1991 elections, the Social Democratic People's Party and the People's Labour Party formed 88 MPs in an alliance under the SHP. 18 MEP-based MPs later resigned from SHP and moved to the DEP. In the 1995 elections, the ANAP and the Grand Union Party formed an alliance under the ANAP and issued 132 MPs. Seven members of the Grand Union Party later resigned and returned to their party. In the 2007 elections, the Republican People's Party and the Democratic Left Party were allied, elected MP from the 13 DSP CHP lists, moved to their party after the election. Finally, the Saadet Party and the Great Union Party allied in the June 7, 2015 elections, but the alliance received 2.06 per cent of the vote and failed to issue lawmakers. In these alliances conducted through fraud against the law, it has been a matter of whether the candidates of political parties will run under the roof of another political party, whether those political parties will leave their parties and their candidates' parties, or whether they will be elected presidents and managers in the extraordinary congress after their return to their political parties, to be nominated because they are not members of the other party. With this proposal of law, the political parties will then form an alliance on a legal and legitimate basis, not through deception against the law. That is the essence of the arrangement. With the alliances made in our country and the implementation of election alliances in other countries, there is an arrangement for the alliance establishment. This arrangement also applies to the principle of justice in the representation, since the electorate will will will be reflected more in parliament through alliances. With this law proposal for each political party, which envisions the same conditions and offers equal opportunities, it allows political parties to form an election alliance in a transparent environment and on legal ground. It also opens the way for honest and consistent politics against the electorate. tbmm-2018-03-12sit06spe0387par0012	Mr. President, honorable lawmakers, I have made a pledge on behalf of myself over the entire bill of law on the basic provisions of election 298 and the amendment to certain laws. I hereby salute your grand delegation, the General Assembly, and our beloved nation. Mr. President, before I speak, I am celebrating the 97th anniversary of the acceptance of our Istiklal anthem, our Istiklal Anthem, and our legend of Çanakkale, who has understood the truths of his era, our national poet Mehmet Akif Ersoy. I call upon our beloved martyrs who have served this country, and I thank and thank our heroic veterans. I hope my Lord doesn't make these people re-write the royal anthems. Besides, you know, it's the anniversary of March 12, 1971. The coup attempt on July 15th, with Allah's permission, was then closed in Turkey -- the period of the coup. Another issue, you know, is to ensure the safety of our southern borders, especially our people, our citizens' lives and goods, to all terror organizations -- including Operation Olive Dallı, including DEAŞ, the PKK, the PSD, the YPG, the security of our nation. May Allah be with them and their help. Mr. President, the high-value lawmakers, the liberal and pluralistic democratic regimes, are based on the free, equal, free and honest elections. Elections show that democratic order is the primary source of legitimacy and that governments are adopted by the public. The right of free elections, which is the basis of democratic governance, has meaning and value with votes of free will, free of any influence. The freedom of elections and the secret voting principles of the Constitution's 67th Article are that elections are carried out in a safe, peaceful environment, and that the election can reflect its free will into the chest without any influence or pressure. The attention on this issue is of great importance to the development of the democratic regime. It is one of the most important tasks of the state to ensure that the elections are held in accordance with the rules of honesty. As part of this mission, additional measures to ensure the safety and neutrality of the election are needed. In this scope, with our proposal, some changes are expected to be made in the Law on the Basic Decisions of Elections 298 and Voters Logs and other electoral laws. With our proposal, it will be decided by the Supreme Election Board to move the polls to the nearest electoral districts where election safety can be met, to unite the poll districts, except in areas that can be difficult to secure election safety, as well as the liberalisation of elections and the secret voting principles of the polls, so that voters can be able to use their choice rights without any influence or pressure. The age of parliamentary election has been reduced from 25 to 18, with amendments made to Article 76 of Law No. 6771 and Constitution. While there is no constitutional obligation, the age of being elected to local administrative bodies is also reduced to 18. However, the age of the muhtars, city council members, city council members and mayors will also be reduced to 18. It thus opens the way for the young population to be represented more in its administration with decision-making bodies in local governments. Instead of identifying the head of the polling board from the well-known and the members of the polling board from among the members of the elders' council and the members of the elders' delegation, other than those reported by political parties, a change is being made to be determined by all public officials who reside in the district. According to this, the mayor of the district election will identify twice as many public officials as the president of the polling board, who are needed by the authorities, prior to the name of the public officials reported on the basis of the settlement, and then identify the property as the president of the polling board, who is not in the presence of the public officials. That will be the choice of the original and reserve members. Dear lawmakers are still putting ballots in different envelopes in the municipal elections and the municipal election elections in the election of municipal administrative bodies. This causes the vote to be invalidated for reasons such as putting ballots for other types of voters in different envelopes or mixing crates for which the envelope would be thrown. The most important example of this is that in the 2014 elections, the envelopes were found in the contents of the ballots and thus the ballots were invalidated. In order to prevent problems that have arisen in practice, the election of local administrative bodies has arranged for all ballots to be placed in the same envelope. The amendment with Law No. 6771 is envisioned to place ballots in the same envelope in the presidential and parliamentary elections, in an effort to prevent similar problems in the election of local administrative bodies. According to the current arrangement, law enforcement can only come to the polls at the call of the head of the polling board or one of the members. The proposal also stipulates that the law enforcement forces will be able to come around the polls after a personal tip by voters. The law enforcement force will leave the polls once the call or tip is removed, as they must follow the polling board's instructions. The proposal also removes bans on the Political Parties Law and election laws, allowing political parties to enter the elections together by making alliances in parliamentary elections, giving them procedures and basis for the joint action of political parties in the elections. Thus, it is possible to establish a legal basis for long-standing operational alliances in our country. In order to ensure election safety, the Supreme Election Board will be able to decide whether the polls are to be moved, election or polling areas are to be merged at the request of the governor or the mayor of the municipal election board. According to this, the decision will be made to move the polls to the nearest electoral districts where election safety can be secured, except for the detention zones -- and to combine the electoral areas -- so that voters may be able to use the rights to vote under no influence or pressure, due to the liberalisation of elections and secret voting principles. Dear friends, dear friends, and, you know, in our elections -- I've been on the election boards a lot, but -- the Supreme Election Board is authorized to use the constitutional right to open the way for those who have been prevented from voting because of their disability. In this scope, the Supreme Election Board will determine the procedures and basis for the process of forming mobile polling boards, using votes, counting and combining them with its transcript. Dear lawmakers, another issue has been the Supreme Election Board's decision that since 1984 it has also decided that it should not be cancelled due to its lack of a ballot seal. For that reason, the polling board seal was not found, but it is decided that the envelopes containing the filigram, the emblem and the municipal election board seal would be valid. According to this, it will not invalidate these envelopes if the Turkish Republic High Election Board filigra and the High Election Board are not only sealed by the authority election boards because of the sealing of the county election board. So, basically, we're predicting a free, equal, free, and honest election with this offer. I wish our proposal would bring blessings to our country, our nation, our political parties and our democracy. I salute you all.

tbmm-2018-03-12sit08spe0657par0390	Mr. President, the elections are important to determine whether a country is a democratic country or an authoritarian country. Just because voters can vote in the chest doesn't make that election democratic. The basic requirement for democracy in the election is that each party in that country can benefit from equal opportunities. In regimes with which the separation between the ruling party and the state has been thinned, where the ruling party and the state use its resources to its advantage indefinitely in the election, the elections are free to vote, with the media, the judiciary and education in absolute control of power. The election rules are obeyed, but the election is not fair because the government uses all the resources of the state to win the election. Such regimes are called competitive authoritarian regimes. There's an apparent rivalry between parties, but the conditions are inequality. Competitive authoritarian regimes have three ways: one, democratisation; two, unstable authoritarianism; three, stable authoritarianism. In Turkey, five or six years ago, we were discussing democratisation, while today we calculate how quickly democracy will crash. Now the country is rapidly becoming a stable and very stable authoritarian regime. So now we're going from the polls to a regime where the elections are questionable, where there's always a state party. Now, a voting seal, which has been printed in the middle of parties C and D, which have not joined the elections under normal circumstances, will be accepted as a non-result, since it is not fully understood which party it was printed, and will be registered in the invalid voting section in the polls. With this arrangement, the fact that a significant portion of these invalid votes are considered valid in favour of the alliance parties would be extremely unjust, and it would discrediting the will of the citizens. Mr. President, we've all testified in the election process that sometimes a political party can win or lose a legislature or a mayor with very small voting differences. Taking into account this reality, it is not possible for us to accept this arrangement to ensure that the votes that should be counted without delay are accepted in favour of the parties that form alliances. Therefore, we feel that this arrangement should be removed from the proposal text. Instead, we propose that the polls only accept the valid votes the parties have received alone, and that the unification of these original votes should be collected by the Alliance's parties as the municipal election boards are drafting their municipal election minutes. Otherwise, it's unfair for political parties to gain parliamentary power through undetermined, undetectable votes that violate the voter's will, which clearly violates the principle of equal voting. With these feelings and thoughts, I salute the General Assembly again.

With the proposal of the law, fundamentally arrangements for election security and election alliances are being made; political freedoms are expanded, the power of voter will is increased in Parliament. Arrangements are made to ensure that our citizens can exercise their right to choose freely and that the voter's will is fully reflected in the chest. These regulations are general and abstract, not to any political party, but to offer all parties the same rights and opportunity, not to violate any constitutional principle. Therefore, claims that the proposal of the law violates the principle of equality of the Constitution do not reflect the facts because parties have an equal right to form alliances or to enter elections alone, which would mean the use of equal opportunities offered by our democracy, regardless of their preference. tbmm-2018-03-12sit11spe0924par0034	Mr. President, I have made a promise regarding the 18th amendment to the 29th amendment of Congressman Election Law 2839. Dear lawmakers, the 18th article actually adds two new concepts to electoral law. I didn't look into it, I don't know if it's in other countries' law or not, but for the first time in our election law, this concept contains an adjustment in the form of "ittifak common votes." Of course, technically, it's an arrangement on how to divide votes into parties, how to distribute them, in connection with the material proposal. Apart from the votes each political party received, it also organises how to divide the votes, which are expressed as the common votes of theitifak. Of course, it's the technical dimension of the job, but the important thing here is the Alliance's joint votes. Where do they come from, how do they vote? Now, when we look at it, it's considered a preferential seal, which has been moved out of the preferential area of any party, for all political parties who have not formed an alliance, who have entered the election on their own; normally, this is the practice. But what we call an alliance is that if more than one party is part of the same alliance, this practice, which is non-stop for other parties, has found here the common votes of ittifak. So if any party has not chosen any party within the alliance or its preference has been moved into the preference area of either party or not into any party, but has been sealed into the framework that frames the alliance, the common votes of the alliance are considered. This is considered primarily an inequality in terms of electoral justice, an unpredictability or an illegal choice for other parties, a legitimate and valid choice for the alliance. Now the logic here is clear, in fact, the logic. Well, that's what we're always saying, the arrangement is about the interests of the alliance. So what we're saying is that the parties that have agreed at the Alliance point, they've been arranged for their own benefit, that's the logic of it: to bag whatever you have, in an alliance bag -- valid, invalid -- votes that are invalid for other parties, but fill it in here. Now, of course, it's the technical dimension of the job, but in terms of law, it's also a serious problem for the choice of choice, because when the voters use their choice, they actually need to be clear, they need to be precise. If the will of the vote is not clearly and precisely reflected in those votes, naturally it is an obstacle to this Parliament as well as to the representational democracy or to the concrete reflection of the will of the people, because it will be reflected in the will through uncertain votes. So aside from all the issues we've always discussed -- the whole alliance logic, the idea, the idea, the crossing of the dam, the finding of 50+1 percent, the crossing of the 10% dam -- it's a system based exclusively on stealing votes, or a system that translates invalid votes in favor of the alliance. Before I put an end to my words, I really congratulate the nationalist movement party's team on this proposal. In addition to an arrangement for the EC PARTY to obtain only 50 percent, 90 per cent of the 26 articles are organised in favour of the MHP, and I congratulate that team freely. I mean, what I mean by the election is that it's going to come out mathematically. I salute you all.
